## 2026-09-30 ~01:55 CDT — Magic Eden legacy-approval exploit verification + scoped Base checks
- Independently verified reports of an active exploit involving stale Limit Break Payment Processor V2 approvals used by Magic Eden’s former EVM marketplace. Relevant V2 operator: `0x9A1D00bEd7CD04BCDA516d721A596eb22Aac6834`; reports say lingering approvals on Ethereum/Polygon/Base and related networks remain dangerous until revoked.
- Read-only Base RPC checks returned zero/false for the specific exposures tested: Looper ERC-721 `isApprovedForAll` from `0x4f9883c7331ba59a56360ffa3c332e0bc09029fc`; Helixa ERC-721 `isApprovedForAll` from Foil `0x6573682faee72a4a96e791ba262439f1df3a268d`; and WETH/USDC allowances from both wallets to the vulnerable V2 operator.
- **Scope correction to the user-facing “you’re clean” wording:** these results cover only those named Base contracts/tokens and the two tested wallets. They do not establish that every NFT collection, chain, token, or the separate Looper-holding wallet `0x72491ba4Ef67a46809C4E79126f4d2Ea2DdecE0A` is clean.

- **UPDATE replacing the old NFT Trencher raffle schema:** raffle rows now live under `/base/raffles` (141 entries observed), while aggregate totals live under `/base/counts`; the cache-busting `?t=` pattern still works. Parsers expecting raffle rows at the top level will miss them.
